# Repro tests

AI-assisted findings, confirmed by automated checks, not yet triaged by a human Lucene committer; this site files nothing upstream.

One directory per public bug: the minimal repro sources, what they show, and how to run them against a Lucene checkout. Rows withheld pending security review have no repro here.

| Bug | Area | Priority | Title |
|---|---|---|---|
| [LFV-001](LFV-001/) | index / IndexWriter NRT | high | openIfChanged returns null and isCurrent stays true after a flush when the only changes are doc-values updates |
| [LFV-016](LFV-016/) | index / doc-values updates | high | deleteDocuments(Query) on a doc-values field is evaluated against stale values when doc-values updates are pending |
| [LFV-044](LFV-044/) | codecs / doc values | high | Doc-values range queries with a skip index return wrong hits when a segment's values span more than Long.MAX_VALUE |
| [LFV-222](LFV-222/) | join / block join (ToParentBlockJoinQuery.BlockJoinBulkScorer) x search / BooleanQuery MUST_NOT bulk scoring (ReqExclBulkScorer, #16669) | high | A MUST_NOT clause next to a scoring ToParentBlockJoinQuery is checked against child documents instead of parents, so excluded parents are returned, and the search throws when a parent/child block straddles a 4096-doc window |
| [LFV-106](LFV-106/) | search / vector similarity queries and the query cache | high | Vector similarity queries report isCacheable == true although their per-segment result depends on live docs, the search timeout and the filter, so a shared query cache serves one reader's or one search's result to another |
| [LFV-038](LFV-038/) | search / multi-term queries | high | Doc-values-rewritten multi-term queries are invisible to QueryVisitor, so they escape the nested clause limit and the query-cache RAM budget |
| [LFV-011](LFV-011/) | index / IndexWriter merge | high | With index sorting and a parent field, deleting only a block's parent makes every later merge fail |
| [LFV-005](LFV-005/) | search / HNSW | high | HNSW merge-time graph reuse can leave the entry node with no neighbours, dropping kNN recall to zero |
| [LFV-811](LFV-811/) | codecs / HNSW vectors on the 10.x line (merge-time graph reuse: InitializedHnswGraphBuilder, MergingHnswGraphBuilder, ConcurrentHnswMerger; Lucene99HnswVectorsWriter.vem metadata) | high | On 10.5.1, 10.5.2 and branch_10_6 (the 10.6.0 RC1 revision), an HNSW merge that reuses a graph written with a different maxConn either fails and closes the IndexWriter (maxConn raised) or commits a segment that no kNN query can search (maxConn lowered) |
| [LFV-158](LFV-158/) | analysis / StandardTokenizer and the other JFlex-generated scanners (zzRefill in both Lucene JFlex skeletons) | medium | StandardAnalyzer and the other JFlex-generated scanners (StandardTokenizer, UAX29URLEmailTokenizer, ClassicTokenizer, WikipediaTokenizer, HTMLStripCharFilter) silently index no tokens for a text value that ends in an unpaired high surrogate, such as a string cut inside an emoji; values longer than the scan buffer lose or split their last buffer window |
| [LFV-146](LFV-146/) | index / FieldInfos and segment metadata strings (DataOutput#writeString in.fnm, segments_N and.si) | medium | Two field names that differ only in an unpaired surrogate are written to disk as the same U+FFFD name, so IndexWriter commits an index that DirectoryReader and a new IndexWriter refuse to open (on main also colliding field attribute keys and commit user-data keys) |
| [LFV-644](LFV-644/) | index / IndexWriter deletes (deleteDocuments, DocumentsWriterDeleteQueue array nodes, per-thread delete slices) | medium | IndexWriter.deleteDocuments(Term...) and deleteDocuments(Query...) keep the caller's array without copying it, so documents still buffered in RAM are deleted by whatever the array holds when the per-thread writer later applies the delete (at its next addDocument or flush): a caller that refills or clears its array after the call returned keeps documents it deleted and deletes documents no call named, silently and also after commit (since 4.0.0) |
| [LFV-904](LFV-904/) | core / index write path (delete-by-query: FrozenBufferedUpdates.applyQueryDeletes) x core / search and queries (reader-dependent rewrites) | medium | IndexWriter.deleteDocuments(Query) and updateDocuments(Query,...) rewrite the query separately on each segment, so reader-dependent queries (kNN, the default FuzzyQuery and other top-N term rewrites, CommonTermsQuery, MoreLikeThisQuery, score ranges) delete a set that depends on the segment layout and differs from what IndexSearcher.search returns |
| [LFV-118](LFV-118/) | index / IndexingChain per-field schema validation (frozen-FieldType cache) | medium | The frozen-FieldType validation cache (#15886) keys a field on its first value's type, so for a field built from several field types a later document skips the per-field schema check (or is checked against stale state after a rejected addBatch), depending on value order, flushes and earlier operations |
| [LFV-009](LFV-009/) | index / doc-values updates | medium | tryUpdateDocValue is overwritten by an earlier, still-buffered doc-values update |
| [LFV-901](LFV-901/) | core / index write path (delete-by-query: FrozenBufferedUpdates.applyQueryDeletes over the ReadersAndUpdates pooled reader) x core / search kNN queries | medium | IndexWriter.deleteDocuments with a kNN query runs on the writer's pooled segment reader, whose live docs miss the writer's own earlier deletes, and on the whole flushed segment including documents added later, so the query picks its k nearest among documents already deleted or added after the call and the acknowledged delete removes fewer documents than it should, often none (since 9.0.0) |
| [LFV-643](LFV-643/) | index / IndexWriter doc-values updates (BinaryDocValuesUpdate, DocumentsWriterDeleteQueue slices, FieldUpdatesBuffer) | medium | IndexWriter.updateBinaryDocValue and updateDocValues(Term, BinaryDocValuesField) keep the caller's BytesRef without copying it, so documents still buffered in RAM get the bytes that BytesRef holds at the writer's next document or flush instead of the value passed to the call (every release since 4.8.0) |
| [LFV-045](LFV-045/) | search / concurrency | medium | IndexSearcher#count hangs forever when Weight#count throws, for example on a query timeout, on a segment split into intra-segment partitions |
| [LFV-596](LFV-596/) | index / IndexWriter frozen update packets vs. merge carry-over (forceApply, commitMergedDeletesAndUpdates, BufferedUpdatesStream) | medium | IndexWriter.forceApply marks a re-resolving update packet finished outside the IndexWriter lock when its re-iteration matches no segment, so a merge that commits in that gap skips the packet's already-resolved doc-values updates and nobody applies them again: an acknowledged updateNumericDocValue/updateDocValues is lost and softUpdateDocument leaves two live versions of the document, also after commit (since 7.0.0) |
| [LFV-002](LFV-002/) | index / IndexWriter merge | medium | Merge doc-values carry-over (#16570) can lose an acknowledged update or close the writer on a duplicate delGen |
| [LFV-010](LFV-010/) | index / soft deletes | medium | A try* un-delete or pending soft-delete change is ignored or lost by later term-based operations |
| [LFV-451](LFV-451/) | index / soft deletes (tryUpdateDocValue un-delete vs. a running merge's carry-over) | medium | An un-delete through IndexWriter#tryUpdateDocValue during a merge of the document's segment returns success and is visible to NRT readers, but the merge commit silently drops the document |
| [LFV-014](LFV-014/) | index / addIndexes | medium | addIndexes publishes segments outside the full-flush boundary, breaking sequence-number ordering |
| [LFV-110](LFV-110/) | index / IndexWriter doc-values update write path (ReadersAndUpdates.writeFieldUpdates, the checkpoint after writeReaderPool, IndexFileDeleter reference counts) | medium | An IOException late in a doc-values update write leaves IndexWriter open but inconsistent: published update files are never checkpointed and get deleted while still referenced, and a failed pooled-reader re-open silently drops the update |
| [LFV-473](LFV-473/) | index / DocumentsWriter full flush (DocumentsWriterFlushControl.abortFullFlushes, blockedFlushes) and the error paths of IndexWriter.getReader and prepareCommit | medium | A non-tragic exception during an NRT reopen or commit full flush aborts the DWPTs other threads filled during that flush, silently losing acknowledged documents and updates while the writer stays open |
| [LFV-843](LFV-843/) | index / IndexWriter full flush (DocumentsWriter.flushAllThreads naked-delete freeze, DocumentsWriterDeleteQueue.close) | medium | An Error (in practice an OutOfMemoryError) while an NRT reopen or commit that has no buffered documents freezes the buffered deletes and doc-values updates is replaced by IllegalStateException("Can't close queue unless all changes are applied") from the finally of DocumentsWriter.flushAllThreads, so IndexWriter stays open instead of closing itself and its next commit silently drops those acknowledged deletes and updates (with -ea an AssertionError replaces the Error; every release since 8.2.0) |
| [LFV-195](LFV-195/) | search / phrase queries (ExactPhraseMatcher maxFreq and merged impacts, PhraseScorer TOP_SCORES pruning) | medium | Exact PhraseQuery/MultiPhraseQuery: when the first query term occurs twice at one document position, the phrase freq exceeds the maxFreq/impacts bound, so Scorer#getMaxScore is below score and TOP_SCORES search drops the best hit |
| [LFV-319](LFV-319/) | queryparser / classic MultiFieldQueryParser (getFieldQuery(String,String,boolean) per-field clause grouping and getMultiTermPositionQuery; core util/QueryBuilder graph phrases) | medium | MultiFieldQueryParser under AND treats clause i of every field's analyzed BooleanQuery as term position i and requires each group, so fields whose analyzers tokenize the same text differently lose (and sometimes gain wrong) matches (released since 6.2.0, default settings since 7.0.0), and since #16446 (unreleased) graph-phrase alternatives such as a multi-word synonym are required together |
| [LFV-242](LFV-242/) | suggest / document (NRT) suggester (NRTSuggester.getMaxTopNSearcherQueueSize via SuggestIndexSearcher and CompletionQuery; Util.TopNSearcher queue depth) | medium | NRTSuggester returns too few or wrongly ranked suggestions in a segment with only a few deleted documents, because its deleted-document queue slack (long)(topN / liveDocsRatio) rounds back down to topN |
| [LFV-225](LFV-225/) | analysis / Thai (ThaiTokenizer#incrementWord skip loop; ThaiAnalyzer and the 'thai' tokenizer SPI) | medium | ThaiTokenizer and ThaiAnalyzer silently drop a number written directly after a period, '#' or currency sign (2567 in 'พ.ศ.2567', 1,500 in 'ราคา ฿1,500', 42 in 'page.42'), because a word segment is skipped whole when its first character is not a letter or digit |
| [LFV-305](LFV-305/) | queryparser / classic QueryParser and MultiFieldQueryParser phrase slop (QueryParserBase.getFieldQuery(String,String,int), MultiFieldQueryParser.getFieldQuery and applySlop; core util/QueryBuilder graph and boosted phrases) | medium | Classic QueryParser and MultiFieldQueryParser ignore an explicit phrase slop ("..."~N) when the phrase analyzes to a token graph (multi-word synonyms, WordDelimiterGraphFilter) or, for QueryParser, carries token boosts, and search with the default phraseSlop instead: fewer hits with the default slop 0, extra hits for an explicit ~0 when a larger default slop is set |
| [LFV-358](LFV-358/) | facet / DrillSideways (sequential DrillSidewaysScorer, drill-down-advance strategy, two-phase drill-down dimensions) | medium | Sequential DrillSideways skips the two-phase matches check of the second-cheapest drill-down dimension on segments without deletions, so documents that fail that dimension but are in its approximation are returned as drill-down hits and counted in the facets (released since 5.3.0) |
| [LFV-789](LFV-789/) | facet / DrillSideways (sequential query-first scoring: score mode of the base weight and the scorable handed to the hit collector) | medium | Sequential DrillSideways lets a TOP_SCORES hit collector prune the shared base query, so drill-sideways facet counts silently miss matching documents |
| [LFV-019](LFV-019/) | util / automaton | medium | RegExp no longer parses an escaped range start such as [\.-z] as a range |
| [LFV-359](LFV-359/) | queryparser / SimpleQueryParser (near operator: consumePhrase phrase slop) | medium | SimpleQueryParser drops the '+', '\|', quote or '(' typed directly after a phrase slop, so "a b"~2+c parses as phrase OR c and "a b"~2\|c (default operator AND) as phrase AND c |
| [LFV-560](LFV-560/) | queryparser / flexible StandardQueryParser (processor pipeline order: WildcardQueryNodeProcessor runs before MultiFieldQueryNodeProcessor) | medium | StandardQueryParser with setMultiFields normalizes field-less prefix and wildcard terms with field null, before the multi-field expansion, so per-field analyzers are ignored for them (and a PerFieldAnalyzerWrapper over Map.of throws NullPointerException) |
| [LFV-204](LFV-204/) | document / geo shape fields (ShapeField triangle encoding; LatLonShape and XYShape CONTAINS queries) | medium | ShapeField.encodeTriangle puts the wrong edge flag on the first edge when it flips a clockwise triangle, so LatLonShape and XYShape CONTAINS queries return wrong hits |
| [LFV-698](LFV-698/) | geo / shape queries (Polygon2D intersectsLine/intersectsTriangle; LatLonShape and XYShape INTERSECTS and DISJOINT with a query polygon that has holes) | medium | Polygon2D.intersectsLine/intersectsTriangle never test the query polygon's hole edges, so LatLonShape/XYShape INTERSECTS misses, and DISJOINT matches, a line or polygon whose vertices all lie in holes but which crosses the polygon between them |
| [LFV-291](LFV-291/) | queries / intervals (Intervals.notContainedBy: NotContainedByIntervalsSource disjunction rewrite, LUCENE-8477) | medium | Intervals.notContainedBy(small, or(B1, B2)) returns small intervals that lie inside B1 or B2, because the default disjunction rewrite turns it into an OR of per-clause filters |
| [LFV-828](LFV-828/) | queries / intervals (overlap filters Intervals.overlapping / nonOverlapping / notWithin vs the LUCENE-8477 disjunction rewrite that containedBy and within apply to their reference) | medium | Intervals.overlapping, nonOverlapping and notWithin read a disjunctive reference through the minimized or, while containedBy and within test every alternative, so for the reference or(york, "new york") the same interval is reported both within and not within n positions of it, and containedBy(new, ref) matches 'new york' where overlapping(new, ref) does not |
| [LFV-287](LFV-287/) | queries / intervals (NotContainingIntervalsSource, Intervals.notContaining) | medium | Intervals.notContaining drops minuend intervals that a subtrahend interval overlaps on the right edge or encloses, though they contain no subtrahend interval |
| [LFV-909](LFV-909/) | queries / span queries (per-document lockstep of the ContainSpans subclasses in SpanContainingQuery and SpanWithinQuery) | medium | SpanContainingQuery and SpanWithinQuery miss documents and spans when the little query's spans end out of order (for example a SpanOrQuery of a longer near clause and a shorter clause that can occur inside it): their single forward pass tests only the current little span (containing) or never goes back to a big span it skipped (within), so a later, shorter little span inside a big span is never tried |
| [LFV-457](LFV-457/) | queries / intervals (Intervals.analyzedText: IntervalBuilder.analyzeGraph; flexible StandardQueryParser fn: interval functions) | medium | Intervals.analyzedText keeps only the first of the tokens stacked at a position whenever the analyzed text is a token graph (a multi-word synonym or a WordDelimiterGraphFilter catenation elsewhere in the text), so interval queries silently miss documents, even documents with exactly the query words (AssertionError under -ea) |
| [LFV-286](LFV-286/) | queries / intervals (Intervals.atLeast: MinimumShouldMatchIntervalsSource) | medium | Intervals.atLeast stops early when the sub-iterator it just used runs out, so it loses minimal intervals and misses documents when nested; the same hand-over also lets a stale sub-iterator re-enter and return a non-minimal interval out of order |
| [LFV-627](LFV-627/) | core / search (NGramPhraseQuery rewrite), on n-gram fields built with analysis-common (NGramTokenizer, CJKAnalyzer / CJKBigramFilter) | medium | NGramPhraseQuery matches documents its own PhraseQuery does not whenever adjacent gram positions do not come from adjacent text: multi-valued n-gram fields with a position increment gap below n (Lucene's default gap is 0), CJKAnalyzer text with a break between CJK runs (punctuation, a digit, a Latin word or a stop word), and NGramTokenizer pre-tokenizing through isTokenChar |
| [LFV-220](LFV-220/) | join / ToParentBlockJoinSortField nested sort (ToParentDocValues missing-child count, #15727) | medium | ToParentBlockJoinSortField treats a parent as having a missing child value whenever its block holds any document outside childFilter, even if every document has the sort field (regression in 10.5.0) |
| [LFV-232](LFV-232/) | grouping / two-pass grouping (TopGroupsCollector second pass, TopGroups.merge, TopGroupsCollectorManager, GroupingSearch) | medium | Grouping with a within-group sort that equals Sort.RELEVANCE but is a different object (new Sort, new Sort(SortField.FIELD_SCORE)) returns the wrong documents per group when results from slices or shards are merged, and since 10.5.0 a NaN GroupDocs.maxScore |
| [LFV-660](LFV-660/) | suggest / document suggester (NRTSuggester skipDuplicates, queue sizing) and util / FST (Util.TopNSearcher) | medium | Document suggester with skipDuplicates=true and an index-time synonym returns fewer than n distinct suggestions (n=2 drops one that n=3 returns), because Util.TopNSearcher drops its queue before the last result although acceptPartialPath can still reject that path, and NRTSuggester sizes the queue from entries per analyzed form, which ignores synonym graph paths |
| [LFV-691](LFV-691/) | suggest / document (NRT) suggester (RegexCompletionQuery automaton intersected with the NRTSuggester FST, including the internal END and dedup bytes; also inside ContextQuery) | medium | RegexCompletionQuery lets '.' and negated character classes match the document suggester's internal end byte (0x00), so a suggestion one character shorter than the pattern is returned ('foo.' returns foo as well as food, 'foo..' returns food) |
| [LFV-700](LFV-700/) | suggest / analyzing: BlendedInfixSuggester result collection | medium | BlendedInfixSuggester loses a distinct suggestion when a duplicate entry arrives while its result set is full: boundedTreeAdd evicts the lowest result before TreeSet.add rejects the duplicate, so lookup returns num-1 suggestions, or a lower-ranked fetched suggestion in place of a higher-ranked one |
| [LFV-254](LFV-254/) | suggest / document (NRT) suggester (ContextQuery: ContextCompletionWeight.setNextMatch boost lookup over ContextSuggestField contexts) | medium | ContextQuery gives an exact context's boost to any longer indexed context that starts with it (exact 'red' boosts a suggestion indexed under 'redwood') when that longer context is matched through a shorter prefix context or addAllContexts, so document-suggester hits get wrong scores and a wrong order |
| [LFV-248](LFV-248/) | suggest / analyzing suggester (AnalyzingSuggester.build, inherited by FuzzySuggester) | medium | AnalyzingSuggester and FuzzySuggester count skipped duplicate copies of a surface form toward maxSurfaceFormsPerAnalyzedForm, so distinct lower-weighted surface forms are silently dropped |
| [LFV-523](LFV-523/) | analysis / TokenStreamToAutomaton (core), reached through ConcatenateGraphFilter (analysis-common) and the completion suggesters (suggest: NRT document suggester, AnalyzingSuggester, FuzzySuggester) | medium | A zero-length token makes TokenStreamToAutomaton drop every path through it, so ConcatenateGraphFilter emits nothing and the document suggester, AnalyzingSuggester and FuzzySuggester silently lose that suggestion (stock trigger: a standalone tatweel U+0640 with ArabicAnalyzer, PersianAnalyzer or SoraniAnalyzer) |
| [LFV-798](LFV-798/) | suggest / document (NRT) suggester (SuggestField's raw-value reserved-character check vs. the analyzed form that CompletionTokenStream passes to NRTSuggesterBuilder's FST encoding) | medium | The document suggester checks its reserved end byte U+0000 only in the raw SuggestField value, not in the analyzed form, so an analyzer that emits U+0000 (for example HTMLStripCharFilter decoding '&amp;#0;') hides another document's suggestion or its own and adds FST inputs out of order at flush (with assertions enabled the IndexWriter closes) |
| [LFV-699](LFV-699/) | suggest / ternary search tree suggester persistence (TSTLookup.store and load, writeRecursively and readRecursively) | medium | TSTLookup.store/load writes each tree node's split char with DataOutput.writeString, so the two surrogate halves of an emoji or CJK Extension B character come back as U+FFFD: after load, get and lookups whose prefix runs through such a character silently find nothing, and some neighbouring BMP keys are lost too (every release since 4.7.0) |
| [LFV-087](LFV-087/) | search / rescoring | medium | RescoreTopNQuery lets deleted documents take top-n slots, and IndexSearcher#count counts them |
| [LFV-315](LFV-315/) | search / rescoring | medium | RescoreTopNQuery builds its inner Weight with ScoreMode.COMPLETE_NO_SCORES, so a values source that needs scores (DoubleValuesSource.SCORES, an expression using _score) reads 0 instead of the inner query's score |
| [LFV-424](LFV-424/) | search / rescoring (SortRescorer; expressions ExpressionRescorer inherits rescore) | medium | SortRescorer and ExpressionRescorer give returned hits another hit's first-pass score when topN is smaller than the number of first-pass hits (AssertionError with -ea) |
| [LFV-582](LFV-582/) | search / IndexSearcher#termStats distributed-statistics hook (SynonymQuery, MultiPhraseQuery, CombinedFieldQuery, SpanWeight) | medium | SynonymQuery, MultiPhraseQuery, CombinedFieldQuery and SpanOrQuery ask IndexSearcher#termStats only about terms that occur in the local reader, so an override that supplies distributed (whole-collection) statistics is never asked about a query term that occurs only on other shards, and documents on those shards score differently than in the whole collection (since 8.3.0, LUCENE-8921) |
| [LFV-190](LFV-190/) | search / similarities and top-k bulk scoring (IndriDirichletSimilarity, TermQuery bulk scorer, BatchScoreBulkScorer) | medium | IndriDirichletSimilarity produces negative scores, and since 10.3.0 a top-k TermQuery search under it silently returns no hits, because BatchScoreBulkScorer passes a hit to the collector only if its score is at least the initial minimum competitive score of 0 |
| [LFV-484](LFV-484/) | search / BlendedTermQuery cross-field statistics blending | medium | Cross-field BlendedTermQuery gives a sparse field's term a docFreq above that field's docCount, so matches score negative (and since 10.3 are dropped from top-k), and a field with no indexed terms makes search throw NullPointerException |
| [LFV-413](LFV-413/) | misc / similarities (SweetSpotSimilarity) with core impacts-based TOP_SCORES pruning | medium | SweetSpotSimilarity with a length plateau (setLengthNormFactors with min &gt;= 2) breaks Similarity's norm-monotonicity contract, so default top-k search silently misses the best hits |
| [LFV-253](LFV-253/) | search / CombinedFieldQuery (BM25F) max-score bound under dynamic pruning (CombinedFieldScorer.getMaxScore as used by MaxScoreBulkScorer) | medium | CombinedFieldQuery computes its max score as score(+Infinity, 1), which is NaN under AxiomaticF2LOG/F2EXP, so top-k pruning silently drops the best hits |
| [LFV-744](LFV-744/) | search / similarities (IndriDirichletSimilarity constructors and the LMSimilarity collection model) | medium | IndriDirichletSimilarity(float mu) uses LMSimilarity's default (cf+1)/(\|C\|+1) collection model instead of IndriCollectionModel (cf/\|C\|), so new IndriDirichletSimilarity(2000f) scores differently from new IndriDirichletSimilarity although both print the same name |
| [LFV-874](LFV-874/) | search / IndriAndQuery (IndriDisjunctionScorer iteration over two-phase clauses) | medium | IndriAndQuery returns documents that match none of its clauses when a clause uses two-phase iteration (PhraseQuery, doc-values range queries), because IndriDisjunctionScorer iterates the clauses' approximations and never calls TwoPhaseIterator.matches |
| [LFV-173](LFV-173/) | search / score fusion (LogOddsFusionQuery: LogOddsFusionWeight#scorerSupplier) | medium | LogOddsFusionQuery returns the raw clause score in segments where only one clause has a scorer, so identical documents score differently depending on their segment and explain disagrees with the score |
| [LFV-443](LFV-443/) | search / score fusion (LogOddsFusionQuery: LogOddsFusionScorer#score with per-signal logit bounds and null weights) | medium | LogOddsFusionQuery with logit bounds but no weights normalizes every clause's logit with the first clause's bounds, so scores disagree with explain and can exceed getMaxScore |
| [LFV-322](LFV-322/) | monitor / presearcher (RegexpQueryHandler custom query handler) | medium | Monitor with RegexpQueryHandler silently misses matching RegexpQuery queries, because the handler indexes each one under a fragment of its pattern text (for example 'cat\|dog', 'colou' or '?mail') that matching terms do not contain |
| [LFV-347](LFV-347/) | monitor / presearcher (RegexpQueryHandler document-side ngram filter SuffixingNGramTokenFilter) | medium | Monitor with RegexpQueryHandler silently misses regexp matches on any short token that contains a supplementary character (an emoji, a CJK Extension B ideograph, a Gothic or Adlam letter), because SuffixingNGramTokenFilter emits no ngrams for that token |
| [LFV-332](LFV-332/) | monitor / query index updates (WritableQueryIndex.commitWithoutNotify and populateQueryCache, reached through both Monitor.register overloads and ConcurrentQueryLoader) | medium | Monitor.register with the same query id twice in one call keeps both versions in the query index, so the query matches a mix of both versions, and after a reopen a persistent Monitor runs the older version while getQuery returns the newer one |
| [LFV-499](LFV-499/) | monitor / query index updates (WritableQueryIndex.commitWithoutNotify failure atomicity, reached through both Monitor.register overloads) | medium | A Monitor.register call that IndexWriter rejects is not rolled back: the earlier versions of the queries in the call are deleted at the next commit or close, and until then getQuery and match disagree |
| [LFV-501](LFV-501/) | monitor / query index updates (WritableQueryIndex.deleteQueries and clear versus commitWithoutNotify, reached through Monitor.deleteById, Monitor.clear and Monitor.register) | medium | Monitor.deleteById and Monitor.clear issue their IndexWriter delete outside the commit lock, so a delete or clear that runs while register is indexing a decomposed query leaves that query partly indexed: it stays registered but silently stops matching what its deleted parts match, also after a reopen |
| [LFV-772](LFV-772/) | monitor / query index updates versus matching (WritableQueryIndex.commitWithoutNotify versus search, reached through Monitor.register and Monitor.match) | medium | Monitor.match running while Monitor.register re-registers the same query id can silently miss that query, because register puts the new version's cache entries into the live cache map before the new index documents are committed and the query index searcher is refreshed |
| [LFV-817](LFV-817/) | search / intra-segment concurrency (index / TermStates lazy per-leaf lookup) | medium | TermStates#get hands a lazily looked-up TermState to the other partitions of the same segment without safe publication, so under intra-segment concurrency a partition can read docFreq or file pointers still at 0 and a non-scoring term or phrase query silently misses matches (executed on aarch64) |
| [LFV-556](LFV-556/) | queries / mlt (MoreLikeThisQuery.rewrite over MoreLikeThis.like(String, Reader...)) x queryparser / xml (LikeThisQueryBuilder) | medium | MoreLikeThisQuery ignores its moreLikeFields and searches only fieldName, so the XML parser's &lt;LikeThisQuery fieldNames="a,b"&gt; searches only the first field (documents similar through the other fields are silently missed, every release since 6.1.0) |
| [LFV-241](LFV-241/) | replicator / NRT segment replication (CopyJob.transferAndCancel, ReplicaNode.newNRTPoint) | medium | After a primary change, an NRT replica keeps a file it already copied from the old primary when the new primary's file has the same name, matching by name only, so the replica serves and commits the wrong documents with CheckIndex clean |
| [LFV-145](LFV-145/) | index / index sorting at merge time (IndexSorter.FloatSorter/DoubleSorter merge keys, MultiSorter) -&gt; search / TopFieldCollector index-sort early termination | medium | Merging segments of an index sorted by a FLOAT or DOUBLE field misplaces a NaN whose sign bit is set, so the merged segment violates its declared index sort (CheckIndex at level 2 or higher reports it) and sorted searches that terminate early on the index sort return wrong top hits |
| [LFV-779](LFV-779/) | index / index sorting at merge time (IndexSorter.IntSorter merge keys, MultiSorter) -&gt; search / TopFieldCollector index-sort early termination | medium | With an INT index sort, merges order documents by the full 64-bit doc value while flush, sorted search and CheckIndex use its low 32 bits, so merging a value outside the int range writes a segment that violates its declared index sort (CheckIndex at level 2 or higher rejects it) and sorted searches that terminate early on the index sort return wrong top hits (a regression in 8.2.0) |
| [LFV-086](LFV-086/) | search / FieldExistsQuery count | medium | FieldExistsQuery#count undercounts documents whose norms-free text field analyzes to no tokens but still has a doc value, so IndexSearcher#count is too low |
| [LFV-008](LFV-008/) | index / soft deletes | medium | Removing a document's soft-delete value brings a hard-deleted document back |
| [LFV-593](LFV-593/) | index / IndexWriter construction from a near-real-time reader (ReaderPool pre-enrollment, PendingSoftDeletes reader constructor, soft-deletes accounting) | medium | With a soft-deletes field configured, an IndexWriter opened from a near-real-time reader's IndexCommit (IndexWriterConfig#setIndexCommit) counts the reader's deletions again, so its next commit can drop a segment that still has live documents, write a commit that cannot be opened, or turn a soft delete into a hard delete (since 7.4.0) |
| [LFV-006](LFV-006/) | util / BKD points | medium | PointInSetQuery silently misses most matches on selectively indexed points (fewer index dimensions than data dimensions) |
| [LFV-475](LFV-475/) | search / points queries (PointInSetQuery segment early exit) | medium | Multi-dimensional PointInSetQuery skips segments that contain matching points, because its segment early exit uses the lexicographically first and last set points as per-dimension bounds |
| [LFV-144](LFV-144/) | search / sorting (NumericComparator doc-values-skipper pruning for FLOAT/DOUBLE) | medium | A FLOAT or DOUBLE sort on a NUMERIC doc-values field with a skip index and no points silently returns wrong or missing top hits when values have the sign bit set (negative values, or -0.0 with a tie-break sort): skipper pruning compares sortable-encoded bounds with the raw IEEE-bit block min/max |
| [LFV-751](LFV-751/) | document / point query factories (BinaryPoint, PointRangeQuery) | medium | BinaryPoint.newExactQuery and the one-dimensional newRangeQuery keep the caller's byte as the query bounds (BinaryPoint.pack returns the caller's array for one dimension), while the same factory packs a private copy for two or more dimensions, so a caller that refills its buffer silently changes queries it already built |
| [LFV-115](LFV-115/) | search / IndexSearcher timeout reporting for vector queries (AbstractKnnVectorQuery rewrite, AbstractVectorSimilarityQuery scorerSupplier) | medium | kNN and vector-similarity searches that hit the IndexSearcher timeout before collecting any hit return 0 hits with IndexSearcher.timedOut == false, so the timed-out search looks like a search without matches |
| [LFV-467](LFV-467/) | search / vector similarity-threshold queries (ExplicitVectorSimilarityCollector with the exhaustive scan and the HNSW graph search) | medium | FloatVectorSimilarityQuery and ByteVectorSimilarityQuery with traversalSimilarity == resultSimilarity (the 3- and 4-argument constructors: the plain ones in 9.10.0-10.4.0, the deprecated Explicit classes in 10.5.2 and main) silently drop documents whose similarity is exactly resultSimilarity, because the collector's minCompetitiveSimilarity rises to the inclusive threshold and every searcher treats an equal score as not competitive |
| [LFV-235](LFV-235/) | index / FilterCodecReader reader lifecycle (IndexReader reference counting) | medium | Closing a FilterCodecReader runs the wrapped reader's close hook directly (in.doClose instead of in.close), so a DirectoryReader that still uses that segment silently finds 0 hits, and its own close later releases the segment a second time and throws |
| [LFV-429](LFV-429/) | index / IndexWriter merge-time reordering (OneMerge#reorder guard; misc BPReorderingMergePolicy) | medium | Reordering merges (BPReorderingMergePolicy) silently break document blocks written before Lucene 9.9, because IndexWriter's reorder guard reads the unrecorded LeafMetaData#hasBlocks flag as 'no blocks' |
| [LFV-490](LFV-490/) | search / FieldExistsQuery and index / FieldInfos field-schema consistency on Lucene-8-created indexes (SegmentInfos per-segment open gate, #14607) | medium | Since 10.4.0 Lucene opens indexes created by Lucene 8 once Lucene 9 has rewritten every segment, but without Lucene 9's field-consistency leniency for them (LUCENE-10518), so FieldExistsQuery returns documents that lack the doc value (or throws) and IndexWriter refuses the index |
| [LFV-278](LFV-278/) | index / addIndexes reorder path: SlowCompositeCodecReaderWrapper merged vector values (MergedDocIterator) over SortingCodecReader inputs | medium | SlowCompositeCodecReaderWrapper's merged vector iterator numbers ordinals by position and ignores each input's own index, so addIndexes of two or more SortingCodecReader inputs through a reordering merge policy writes vectors onto the wrong documents |
| [LFV-420](LFV-420/) | codecs / quantized vector formats merge (Lucene104 scalar-quantized, Lucene102 binary-quantized) with index / addIndexes through SlowCodecReaderWrapper and memory / MemoryIndex | medium | Merging into a Lucene104 scalar-quantized FLOAT32 vector field that does not use COSINE centers the merge source's own vector arrays in place, so IndexWriter.addIndexes changes a source that keeps its arrays (MemoryIndex, FloatVectorValues.fromFloats) and can store wrong full-precision vectors or wrong quantized codes |
| [LFV-491](LFV-491/) | facet / taxonomy (TaxonomyMergeUtils.merge, OrdinalMappingLeafReader) | medium | TaxonomyMergeUtils.merge / OrdinalMappingLeafReader do not re-map the taxonomy ordinals stored in association facet fields, so merged IntAssociationFacetField / FloatAssociationFacetField values are credited to unrelated categories (10.x, main) or lost (9.x) |
| [LFV-722](LFV-722/) | facet / taxonomy (TaxonomyMergeUtils.merge, OrdinalMappingLeafReader; FacetsConfig default DimConfig) | medium | TaxonomyMergeUtils.merge (OrdinalMappingLeafReader) does not re-map the facet ordinals stored in the index field chosen by an overridden FacetsConfig#getDefaultDimConfig, so merged documents silently point at other categories |
| [LFV-284](LFV-284/) | queryparser / classic QueryParser and MultiFieldQueryParser (QueryParser.jj MultiTerm run grouping, QueryParserBase.addClause conjunction step) | medium | Classic QueryParser with the default splitOnWhitespace=false: since #16446 an AND/OR that directly follows a token the analyzer drops (such as a stopword) binds to the whole preceding run of plain terms instead of the last term, so typing the stopword changes which documents match (unreleased regression on main and branch_10_6) |
| [LFV-860](LFV-860/) | search / index-sort range-query shortcuts (Sort.getPrimarySortField; SortedNumericDocValuesRangeQuery, SortedSetDocValuesRangeQuery, IndexSortSortedNumericDocValuesRangeQuery) | medium | Sort.getPrimarySortField (new in 10.6.0, #16424) skips an index-sort field that the reader's FieldInfos do not list, or whose skip index holds a single value, even when that field still orders the documents (a FilterLeafReader hides it but keeps the index sort, or a BinarySortField derives its key from other fields), so doc-values range queries on the next sort field, LongField.newRangeQuery included, return wrong hits or throw |

| [LFV-628](LFV-628/) | search / SynonymQuery pseudo-term statistics (SynonymWeight; reached through QueryBuilder with stock synonym parsers) | medium | SynonymQuery with a repeated term adds that term's totalTermFreq once per copy, so the pseudo-term statistics it gives the Similarity can exceed the field's sumTotalTermFreq (AssertionError in SimilarityBase with -ea, scores from impossible statistics with -da) |
| [LFV-093](LFV-093/) | misc / BP merge-time reordering | medium | Merges through BPReorderingMergePolicy with BpVectorReorderer break document blocks even when a parent field is configured |
| [LFV-211](LFV-211/) | queries / FunctionScoreQuery block-max pruning (#16431) | medium | FunctionScoreQuery's block-max bound multiplies by the wrapped query's no-score max score (0 for a TermQuery), so a BooleanQuery that contains it can drop the true top hits |
| [LFV-435](LFV-435/) | search / TermQuery equality and BooleanQuery clause de-duplication (blended and fuzzy TermStates) | medium | TermStates#equals compares only docFreq, so BooleanQuery still merges blended or fuzzy TermQuery clauses whose totalTermFreq differs, and similarities that read totalTermFreq then score them wrongly (the part of the #10309 fix, #12354, that was left out; main only) |
| [LFV-471](LFV-471/) | index / IndexWriter sequence numbers (tryDeleteDocument, tryUpdateDocValue vs commit) | medium | tryDeleteDocument / tryUpdateDocValue running while commit flushes returns a sequence number above the commit's, yet its change is in that commit |
| [LFV-109](LFV-109/) | index / DirectoryReader reopen with doc-values updates (generation numbers reused across writer sessions) | medium | A reopened reader returns the doc values of a discarded writer session or commit when a later writer session reuses the same doc-values generation number |
| [LFV-430](LFV-430/) | index / IndexWriter tryDeleteDocument (dropDeletedSegment, change accounting) | medium | If tryDeleteDocument removes a segment's last live document and closing that segment's pooled reader throws, IndexWriter stays open but never counts the change, so NRT readers hide the document while commit and close keep it |
| [LFV-452](LFV-452/) | index / IndexWriter deleteAll (change accounting on the exception path, ReaderPool.dropAll) | medium | If closing a pooled segment reader throws inside IndexWriter.deleteAll, the segments are already cleared but the change is never counted: the writer stays open and its NRT readers show an empty index, yet hasUncommittedChanges is false and commit and close keep every document, until a later unrelated commit silently applies the failed deleteAll |
| [LFV-474](LFV-474/) | index / IndexWriter deleteAll versus a concurrent segment flush (DocumentsWriter.lockAndAbortAll, flush-ticket queue) | medium | IndexWriter.deleteAll misses a segment flush that another thread is already running, so documents added before deleteAll survive it and are committed |
| [LFV-590](LFV-590/) | index / IndexWriter forced merge (forceMerge wait loop, mergeExceptions and mergeGen, tragic events) | medium | IndexWriter.forceMerge(n) can return normally with more than n segments when a merge that was already pending or running at the call fails: forceMerge adopts that merge without updating its mergeGen, so addMergeException drops the failure, and the tragic event is published only after mergeFinish has woken the waiting forceMerge |
| [LFV-635](LFV-635/) | index / IndexWriter doc-values updates vs deleteAll (writer-wide field schema, FieldInfos.FieldNumbers) | medium | A doc-values update racing IndexWriter.deleteAll can be queued after deleteAll has cleared its field from the writer's field schema; applying it then makes every commit, NRT reopen and close fail (a tragic close with assertions on), or, if a document added in between gives the field another doc-values type, commits a segment whose field cannot be read |
| [LFV-842](LFV-842/) | index / IndexWriter merge registration (registerMerge, pendingMerges, mergingSegments) | medium | If the IndexWriter InfoStream throws inside registerMerge, the merge stays queued but its segments are not marked as merging, so the next merge selection picks the same segments again and, with assertions disabled, forceMerge commits duplicated documents (every release since 3.2.0) |
| [LFV-013](LFV-013/) | index / doc-values updates | medium | Concurrent doc-values updates can make a merge throw NullPointerException and close the writer |
| [LFV-838](LFV-838/) | index / IndexWriter doc-values updates (BinaryDocValuesFieldUpdates value buffer, FrozenBufferedUpdates apply, applyUpdatesPacket tragic close) | medium | A binary doc-values update whose term matches many documents copies its value once per matched document into one int-sized buffer when it is applied, so at 2^31 bytes in one segment (about 2^30 for the first update of a field in a packet) commit throws ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException (AssertionError with -ea) and IndexWriter closes itself, losing the update and every uncommitted document (every release since 4.8.0) |
| [LFV-652](LFV-652/) | core / index (ParallelLeafReader stored fields x StoredFieldsWriter.MergeVisitor, on addIndexes and wrapForMerge copies) | medium | Copying a ParallelLeafReader into an index (IndexWriter.addIndexes over SlowCodecReaderWrapper, or a merge whose OneMerge.wrapForMerge returns one) silently stores a later sub-reader's stored fields under another field's name: ParallelLeafReader.storedFields lets each sub-reader visit with its own FieldInfo numbers while getFieldInfos renumbers the fields, and StoredFieldsWriter.MergeVisitor skips its remap-by-name step when the numbers look aligned |
| [LFV-018](LFV-018/) | index / readers | medium | ParallelLeafReader.termVectors no longer honours 'the first reader having the field wins' |

| [LFV-707](LFV-707/) | analysis-smartcn / HHMM segmenter character classes (Utility.getCharType -&gt; HHMMSegmenter DELIMITER token -&gt; SegTokenFilter ',' -&gt; default stop list; SmartChineseAnalyzer and HMMChineseTokenizer) | medium | SmartChineseAnalyzer silently drops letters and letter-numbers as if they were punctuation, among them the Chinese zero U+3007 (二〇〇八年 -&gt; 二, 八, 年) and the Roman numerals Ⅰ-Ⅻ (第Ⅲ章 and 第Ⅳ章 analyze identically), because Utility.getCharType classifies four whole Unicode blocks as DELIMITER |

| [LFV-665](LFV-665/) | analysis-common / Arabic light stemmer (ArabicStemmer.stemPrefix; ArabicStemFilter, ArabicAnalyzer and the 'arabicStem' factory) | medium | ArabicStemmer removes at most one prefix, so after it strips the conjunction waw it never strips the article that follows (wa+bi-al-, wa+li-l-, wa+ka-al-, wa+fa-al-), unlike the light10 algorithm ArabicAnalyzer documents |

| [LFV-734](LFV-734/) | analysis / StandardTokenizer JFlex word rule (the &lt;ALPHANUM&gt; rule of core StandardTokenizerImpl.jflex; the same rule in analysis-common UAX29URLEmailTokenizerImpl.jflex) | medium | StandardTokenizer (StandardAnalyzer's tokenizer) and UAX29URLEmailTokenizer misapply the UAX #29 Hebrew quote rules WB7a-WB7c next to other word characters: a Hebrew letter + ASCII apostrophe swallows a following digit or underscore (BET'2 is one term), an ASCII apostrophe after a Hebrew letter that ends a mid-word chain is dropped (HE.SHIN' -&gt; HE.SHIN), and a word with an ASCII quotation mark between Hebrew letters is split when it also contains a mid-word '.', ':' or quote (ALEF.TSADI"LAMED, TAV"ALEF.BET) |

| [LFV-311](LFV-311/) | analysis / HTMLStripCharFilter (JFlex grammar: SCRIPT_COMMENT, STYLE_COMMENT, SINGLE_/DOUBLE_QUOTED_STRING and the %eof exclude list; also the 'htmlStrip' char filter SPI) | medium | HTMLStripCharFilter silently drops all text after a &lt;!-- --&gt;-wrapped &lt;script&gt; or &lt;style&gt; block whose content has an unbalanced quote (for example the apostrophe in a JS comment '// Don't remove' or a CSS comment '/* client's fix */'): the quote opens a quoted-string state that ignores '--&gt;' and the end tag, and at end of input everything after the quote is removed, including the text after the closed &lt;/script&gt; or &lt;/style&gt; |

| [LFV-897](LFV-897/) | analysis-common / compound hyphenation (TernaryTree insert and compact via HyphenationTree.loadPatterns and HyphenationCompoundWordTokenFilter) | medium | Hyphenation grammars whose TernaryTree key array grows past 65,535 chars load without error but silently lose or corrupt patterns, because TernaryTree stores key-array offsets in 16-bit chars (the Norwegian OFFO grammars the Lucene docs point to: nb/nn 1,158 of 27,448 patterns wrong, 1.2 no.xml 309 of 26,017) |

| [LFV-525](LFV-525/) | analysis / pattern tokenizers (analysis-common: SimplePatternTokenizer, SimplePatternSplitTokenizer and their simplePattern / simplePatternSplit factories) | medium | SimplePatternTokenizer and SimplePatternSplitTokenizer stop scanning when a non-matching lookahead reaches the end of the input, so later tokens are dropped (or later separators ignored) |

| [LFV-657](LFV-657/) | analysis-icu / segmentation (ICUTokenizer with DefaultICUTokenizerConfig; break rules Default.rbbi -&gt; Default.brk) | medium | ICUTokenizer silently drops Korean words when a Hangul syllable is followed by a combining mark, soft hyphen, ZWNJ, ZWJ or U+FEFF: the Hangul rules of Default.rbbi do not attach the trailing Extend/Format character, so the segment gets rule status 0 and is skipped as a non-word (U+D55C U+AD6D U+00AD U+C5B4 keeps only U+C5B4; since 4.7.0, LUCENE-4381) |
| [LFV-668](LFV-668/) | analysis-common / Sorani normalizer (SoraniNormalizer.normalize; SoraniNormalizationFilter, SoraniAnalyzer and the 'soraniNormalization' factory) | medium | SoraniNormalizer checks for a word-final HEH before it removes trailing directional marks, so a word ending in HEH followed by RLM, LRM or ALM keeps HEH (U+0647) instead of becoming AE (U+06D5) as the class javadoc says |
| [LFV-728](LFV-728/) | analysis-smartcn / HHMM segmenter character classes (Utility.getCharType SPACE_LIKE test -&gt; HHMMSegmenter OTHER branch -&gt; one-character STRING term; SmartChineseAnalyzer and HMMChineseTokenizer) | medium | SmartChineseAnalyzer indexes whitespace characters as terms: VERTICAL TAB, FORM FEED, OGHAM SPACE MARK and the eleven spaces U+2000-U+200A (EN QUAD to HAIR SPACE, among them EN, EM and THIN SPACE) each become a one-character term, because Utility.getCharType classifies them OTHER instead of SPACE_LIKE |
| [LFV-849](LFV-849/) | analysis / kuromoji completion romanization (romaji_map.txt read by KatakanaRomanizer; JapaneseCompletionFilter, JapaneseCompletionAnalyzer) | medium | Japanese completion suggester (JapaneseCompletionAnalyzer / JapaneseCompletionFilter): words with a small tsu before shi, chi or tsu (雑誌, 決して, マッチ, 三つ) are never suggested for their Hepburn or input-method spellings zasshi, kesshite, macchi/matchi, mittsu, because romaji_map.txt maps ッシ only to 'ssi', ッチ only to 'tti' and ッツ only to 'ttu', while the base rows シ/チ/ツ list shi/chi/tsu and the neighbouring rows ッシャ and ッチャ do double the consonant ('ssha', 'ccha') |
| [LFV-047](LFV-047/) | codecs / vectors | medium | Two antipodal COSINE vectors make the OSQ centroid zero, so flush or merge fails and the writer closes |
| [LFV-046](LFV-046/) | codecs / vectors | medium | COSINE vectors whose float32 squared norm underflows pass validation and give Inf/NaN scores or poison OSQ segments |
| [LFV-183](LFV-183/) | codecs / postings writer and reader (Lucene104 level-1 skip data, ImpactLength; the same field in the Lucene912, Lucene101 and Lucene103 formats) | medium | Since 9.12.0 the default postings writer stores the byte length of a level-1 block's competitive impacts in a signed short, so with a custom Similarity whose norms do not fit in a byte, a term with 8,192 or more postings in one segment (4,096 before 10.4) can need more than 32,767 bytes: flush fails an assertion with -ea, and with -da the segment is written but that term's postings then throw on every read or are read back wrong |
| [LFV-097](LFV-097/) | index / columnar addBatch doc values (dense NUMERIC and SORTED_NUMERIC writers) | medium | When a dense LongColumn's values cursor throws during addBatch, the NUMERIC/SORTED_NUMERIC doc values it already delivered stay buffered with no document, so later documents of that field in the same segment are stored with another document's value |
| [LFV-101](LFV-101/) | index / columnar addBatch (dense points, PointValuesWriter) | medium | When IndexWriter#addBatch fails part-way through a dense points column, the point bytes it already copied stay in the points buffer, so documents added later to the same in-memory segment are silently indexed with the rejected batch's point values |
| [LFV-096](LFV-096/) | index / columnar addBatch (term vectors) | medium | An addBatch that registers a new term-vector field but frames no rows leaves a segment whose FieldInfos declare term vectors with no term-vector files, so the committed index cannot be opened |
| [LFV-094](LFV-094/) | index / columnar addBatch (DictionaryColumn SORTED_SET doc values, SortedSetDocValuesWriter) | medium | A SORTED_SET DictionaryColumn tuple that addBatch rejects (out-of-range ordinal, or ordValue throws) leaves an empty document in the doc-values buffer, so the next flush fails and closes the IndexWriter, or with -da commits a segment whose field cannot be read |
| [LFV-579](LFV-579/) | codecs / vectors writers (Lucene99HnswVectorsWriter and Lucene104ScalarQuantizedVectorsWriter field writers over a flat field writer; columnar addBatch DENSE vector bulk path) | medium | When IndexWriter#addBatch rejects a vector part-way through a DENSE vector column, the core HNSW and scalar-quantized vectors writers fall out of step with a flat field writer that keeps the default addDenseValues (new in #16734): the next commit closes the IndexWriter, or with assertions off commits a corrupt HNSW graph or misaligned COSINE quantization state |
| [LFV-199](LFV-199/) | index / deletion policies (PersistentSnapshotDeletionPolicy save-file loading, crash recovery) | medium | PersistentSnapshotDeletionPolicy adopts a broken newest save file on load and deletes the good older one, so an OS crash, power loss or process kill while snapshot or release writes its save file silently drops every earlier persisted snapshot, and IndexWriter then deletes the snapshotted commits |
| [LFV-521](LFV-521/) | facet / taxonomy writer (DirectoryTaxonomyWriter.addCategory exception safety: cacheIsComplete and reader-manager freshness after a failed near-real-time reader open) | medium | DirectoryTaxonomyWriter gives an existing category a second ordinal after an addCategory call fails with an IOException while the writer opens its near-real-time reader, because the cache stays marked complete or the reader stays stale |
| [LFV-408](LFV-408/) | misc / index tools (IndexSplitter.split) over core / index: SegmentCommitInfo per-commit doc-values update state | medium | IndexSplitter.split writes each copied segment into the destination commit without its doc-values update metadata, so on main the copy silently reads wrong doc values, and in every release since 4.6.0 the first IndexWriter opened on the copy deletes the copied update files and the copy can no longer be opened |
| [LFV-617](LFV-617/) | core index / UpgradeIndexMergePolicy and IndexUpgrader with OneMergeWrappingMergePolicy delegates (SoftDeletesRetentionMergePolicy) | medium | UpgradeIndexMergePolicy rewrites the old segments its delegate declines with its own plain OneMerge, which a wrapping delegate never sees, so IndexUpgrader (or UpgradeIndexMergePolicy) with a SoftDeletesRetentionMergePolicy inside silently purges the soft-deleted documents the retention policy must keep |
| [LFV-481](LFV-481/) | facet / taxonomy (ReindexingEnrichedDirectoryTaxonomyWriter.reindexWithNewOrdinalData) | medium | ReindexingEnrichedDirectoryTaxonomyWriter.reindexWithNewOrdinalData rebuilds only the last committed taxonomy, so categories added since that commit are dropped and their ordinals are later reused for other categories |
| [LFV-520](LFV-520/) | facet / taxonomy writer (DirectoryTaxonomyWriter.addCategoryDocument with the ordinal-document enrichment hook: ReindexingEnrichedDirectoryTaxonomyWriter / enrichOrdinalDocument) | medium | DirectoryTaxonomyWriter gives every later category a wrong ordinal after IndexWriter rejects one enriched ordinal document: the rejected document keeps its docID but the writer's ordinal counter does not advance |
| [LFV-280](LFV-280/) | codecs / per-field postings format merge (PerFieldPostingsFormat attribute bookkeeping) | medium | PerFieldPostingsFormat merge leaves stale inherited format attributes on a field that has no postings left after a segment written by a non-per-field postings codec (for example SimpleTextCodec), so the merged segment cannot be opened |
| [LFV-249](LFV-249/) | codecs / SharedTermsUniformSplit postings format (STUniformSplitTermsWriter.merge under PerFieldPostingsFormat and PerFieldMergeState) | medium | SharedTermsUniformSplit merges silently drop the postings of fields that a source segment stored under another postings format or another shared-terms group (after a per-field postings mapping change, or addIndexes from an index with another mapping), because STUniformSplitTermsWriter.merge reads only the shared-terms block of the first field with terms in each source segment |
| [LFV-244](LFV-244/) | core / codecs: term vectors (TermVectorsWriter.addAllDocVectors merge copier; Lucene90 compressing term-vector writer and reader), reached through IndexWriter.addIndexes(CodecReader) | medium | addIndexes(CodecReader) from a reader whose term vectors list a field with zero terms writes a term-vector chunk that the default codec cannot read back, and every later merge that re-encodes the segment closes the IndexWriter |
| [LFV-417](LFV-417/) | misc / BP merge-time reordering (BPIndexReorderer with document blocks) | medium | BPIndexReorderer assumes every segment ends with a parent document: once a segment ends with child documents that have no parent after them, each BP reordering merge in which that segment comes last throws and closes the IndexWriter |
| [LFV-563](LFV-563/) | index / IndexWriter document blocks (parent field) and merges | medium | A document block rejected at a child can be flushed as a fully deleted segment without the parent field; if the merge policy keeps it (SoftDeletesRetentionMergePolicy can), merges that combine it only with segments without multi-document blocks throw 'expected parent doc field... across all segments' and close IndexWriter, again after every reopen |
| [LFV-256](LFV-256/) | codecs / per-field doc values and vectors formats (PerFieldDocValuesFormat, PerFieldKnnVectorsFormat attribute bookkeeping) | medium | PerFieldDocValuesFormat and PerFieldKnnVectorsFormat accept a per-field hook that returns another per-field format (for example the default codec's docValuesFormat or knnVectorsFormat); the nested writer overwrites the outer per-field attributes, so the committed segment cannot be opened, while PerFieldPostingsFormat rejects the same configuration |
| [LFV-615](LFV-615/) | core index / IndexUpgrader (command line and default-config upgrade path); IndexWriter index-sort validation | medium | IndexUpgrader's command line and default constructors use a bare IndexWriterConfig, so upgrading a sorted index silently and permanently removes its index sort (the application's sorted IndexWriter then refuses to open the index), and an index with a parent field or a soft-deletes field cannot be upgraded at all |
| [LFV-580](LFV-580/) | index / IndexWriterConfig and IndexWriter configuration (creation-only settings re-read from the caller's config object) | medium | IndexWriterConfig setters called after the IndexWriter was created still change that writer (index sort, parent field, soft-deletes field, codec, similarity,...), although the class javadoc says such changes have no effect; a late index sort or parent field makes merges fail and closes the writer, and a late soft-deletes field can drop a live document |
| [LFV-760](LFV-760/) | index / index sorting (IndexingChain index-sort field validation and flush-time sort) | medium | With an index sort, a document whose sort field is indexed without doc values (for example only as a StringField) is accepted by addDocument, and the next flush throws IllegalStateException and closes the IndexWriter, losing every uncommitted document (since 8.6.0) |
| [LFV-787](LFV-787/) | suggest / document suggester (CompletionFieldsConsumer type check at flush and merge; core flush and merge abort paths) | medium | Mixing SuggestField and ContextSuggestField under one field name is accepted by addDocument and rejected only when the completion postings are written at flush or merge, where the IllegalArgumentException is tragic: the IndexWriter closes and loses every uncommitted document, and two cleanly committed segments of the two types make every merge that combines them close the writer again, also in a new writer (released since 5.3.0) |
| [LFV-759](LFV-759/) | index / IndexWriter doc-values updates with index sorting (BinarySortField, custom IndexSorter) | medium | IndexWriter accepts doc-values updates of a field that a custom index-sort key reads (such as a BinarySortField subclass that derives its key from another doc-values field), so the segment keeps its old order while still declaring the index sort: CheckIndex rejects it, sorted searches that terminate early return wrong top hits, and merges carry the disorder forward |
| [LFV-730](LFV-730/) | index / IndexWriter merge soft-delete accounting (mergeMiddle carry-over of soft deletes x OneMerge#wrapForMerge, OneMergeWrappingMergePolicy, SoftDeletesRetentionMergePolicy) | medium | With a soft-deletes field configured, IndexWriter's merge assumes OneMerge#wrapForMerge only revives documents: for each merged segment without hard deletes, every live document that a merge wrapper hides (which the javadoc allows) lowers the new segment's soft-delete count by one, so the merge throws 'invalid softDelCount=-1' when nothing is revived, and under SoftDeletesRetentionMergePolicy commits a too-low count (numDocs and IndexSearcher#count over-count, CheckIndex rejects it, later merges of it throw); regression since 7.7.0 (LUCENE-8569) |
| [LFV-726](LFV-726/) | index / IndexWriter addIndexes(CodecReader...) accounting (reserveDocs, addIndexesReaderMerge soft-delete count) x MergePolicy#findMerges(CodecReader...) / OneMerge#wrapForMerge | medium | addIndexes(CodecReader...) takes the reserved document count and the new segment's soft-delete count from the readers before OneMerge#wrapForMerge, so a merge whose wrapper removes documents over-reserves documents and, with soft deletes, commits a wrong soft-delete count that can drop live documents or corrupt a later merge (since 9.9.0) |
| [LFV-161](LFV-161/) | search / CachingCollector first-pass caching (CachingCollector, CachingCollectorManager) | medium | CachingCollector and CachingCollectorManager report isCached true (no overflow) but hold only the hits their wrapped first-pass collector let through (TOP_SCORES pruning, early leaf termination, skipped leaves), so replay silently feeds the second pass fewer matches than re-running the query |
| [LFV-765](LFV-765/) | facet / sampling (RandomSamplingFacetsCollector.createManager reduce, FacetsCollectorManager.reduceMatchingDocs) | medium | RandomSamplingFacetsCollector.createManager samples the hits twice when a search has more than one slice: the reduce reads each slice's already-sampled getMatchingDocs as its original hits, so the sample is far too small and every amortized facet count is biased low (regression from #13542, 10.0.0 onward) |
| [LFV-303](LFV-303/) | facet / range facet counts (LongRangeCounter multi-valued path, exclusive and overlapping counters) | medium | Multi-valued range facet counts drop matches when a document's values do not arrive in ascending order (custom MultiLongValuesSource/MultiDoubleValuesSource, or raw-bit doubles with two or more negative values): LongRangeCounter.addMultiValued assumes sorted values |
| [LFV-151](LFV-151/) | facet / range and range-on-range facet counts (FacetResult.value with a fastMatchQuery) | medium | Range facet counters add every hit the fastMatchQuery rejects to FacetResult.value, so the dimension total is too high and depends on the segment layout |
| [LFV-727](LFV-727/) | facet / taxonomy facets (TaxonomyFacets#getTopChildrenForPath and #getAllChildren: FacetResult.value of a sub-path; FastTaxonomyFacetCounts, TaxonomyFacetFloatAssociations with a DoubleValuesSource) | medium | Taxonomy facets: for a sub-path of a single-valued hierarchical dimension, FacetResult.value from getTopChildren/getAllChildren is the sum of the children, so documents labeled at the sub-path itself are left out, while getSpecificValue, drill-down and SortedSetDocValues faceting count them |
| [LFV-433](LFV-433/) | facet / LongValueFacetCounts (count-all constructors with a LongValuesSource or MultiLongValuesSource) | medium | LongValueFacetCounts count-all constructors that take a LongValuesSource or MultiLongValuesSource count deleted documents, although they promise the result of a MatchAllDocsQuery (LUCENE-10070 fixed only the field-based constructor) |
| [LFV-437](LFV-437/) | facet / sampling (RandomSamplingFacetsCollector: sampling rate vs bin size in amortizeFacetCounts and getSamplingRate) | medium | RandomSamplingFacetsCollector samples one hit per (int) (1 / samplingRate) hits but amortizes by the untruncated samplingRate, so amortized facet counts are up to 2x too high (1999 hits with sampleSize 1000: every hit is sampled, yet every count is doubled) |
| [LFV-539](LFV-539/) | facet / taxonomy association facets (TaxonomyFacetFloatAssociations value-source path, FloatTaxonomyFacets#updateValueFromRollup count roll-up) | medium | TaxonomyFacetFloatAssociations with a DoubleValuesSource reports a too-high LabelAndValue.count for every label that has matching documents below it in a hierarchical dimension, because FloatTaxonomyFacets rolls the counts up again at every ancestor level (values are right; every release since 9.11.0) |
| [LFV-234](LFV-234/) | grouping / GroupingSearch (all-groups and all-group-heads passes) | medium | GroupingSearch.setIgnoreDocsWithoutGroupField(true) is applied only to the first pass, so totalGroupCount, getAllMatchingGroups and getAllGroupHeads still include the null group |
| [LFV-414](LFV-414/) | grouping / range group selectors (LongRangeGroupSelector, DoubleRangeGroupSelector) on scorer-less collection paths: the GroupingSearch score-less cache replay and the DistinctValuesCollector value selector | medium | LongRangeGroupSelector and DoubleRangeGroupSelector load their per-segment values only in setScorer, so grouping with score-less caching returns wrong groups (8.6.0-10.5.2) or throws NullPointerException (main), and DistinctValuesCollector with a range value selector always throws NullPointerException |
| [LFV-884](LFV-884/) | grouping / GroupSelector score needs (FirstPass, SecondPass/TopGroups, AllGroups, AllGroupHeads collector score modes) | medium | Grouping collectors never ask for scores on behalf of their group selector, so DoubleRangeGroupSelector or LongRangeGroupSelector over a score-dependent source (e.g. DoubleValuesSource.SCORES) groups every hit by the placeholder score of a non-scoring search (0 in every repro) when no sort or reducer needs scores, or when AllGroups/AllGroupHeads runs alone (since 8.6.0) |
| [LFV-324](LFV-324/) | facet / SortedSetDocValues faceting (DefaultSortedSetDocValuesReaderState: the per-dimension DimTree and OrdRange built from the field's ordinals in term order, shared by SortedSetDocValuesFacetCounts and ConcurrentSortedSetDocValuesFacetCounts) | medium | SortedSetDocValues faceting builds its facet tree from the byte order of the encoded paths, so a label or dimension name that equals a sibling's name followed by a character below U+001F (TAB, CR, LF, or the U+001E that FacetsConfig writes when it escapes U+001E/U+001F) moves a label's children under that sibling, or drops a whole dimension or one of its labels |
| [LFV-597](LFV-597/) | facet / SortedSetDocValues faceting (FacetsConfig.processSSDVFacetFields path encoding with DELIM_CHAR U+001F / ESCAPE_CHAR U+001E, decoded by DefaultSortedSetDocValuesReaderState) | medium | FacetsConfig writes the bare dimension term of a flat multiValued + requireDimCount SortedSetDocValues dimension without escaping it, so a dimension name containing U+001F or U+001E is read back as a different path: the dimension loses one label and reports that label's count as its own, a phantom dimension appears, a name with two U+001F makes facet-state construction throw for the whole field, and (-ea) a name ending in U+001E throws AssertionError |
| [LFV-541](LFV-541/) | facet / taxonomy writer (DirectoryTaxonomyWriter category identity: writer cache key vs taxonomy index term, perhapsFillCache) | medium | DirectoryTaxonomyWriter indexes a facet label that contains an unpaired surrogate a second time, under a new ordinal, once its writer cache has been filled from the taxonomy index, because the cache compares the label's Java string while the index stores its UTF-8 term with the surrogate replaced by U+FFFD; the taxonomy then holds two categories with one path and facet counts for that value are split |
| [LFV-368](LFV-368/) | facet / getTopDims of taxonomy and SortedSetDocValues facets (TaxonomyFacets#getTopDims and AbstractSortedSetDocValueFacetCounts#getTopDims, shared by FastTaxonomyFacetCounts, the taxonomy int and float association facets, SortedSetDocValuesFacetCounts and ConcurrentSortedSetDocValuesFacetCounts) | medium | Facets#getTopDims on taxonomy and SortedSetDocValues facets admits a multi-valued dimension without requireDimCount even when the query has no hits in it, so the result list contains null (AssertionError with -ea) and a dimension that has hits can be pushed out (released since 9.2.0) |
| [LFV-363](LFV-363/) | facet / taxonomy facets (TaxonomyFacets#getTopDims, shared by FastTaxonomyFacetCounts and the int and float association facets) | medium | Taxonomy Facets#getTopDims returns another dimension's children, childCount and value under a multi-valued dimension's name, because replacing the bottom of the top-dims queue updates dim and value but not dimOrd (released since 9.2.0) |
| [LFV-524](LFV-524/) | facet / MultiFacets and DrillSideways results (MultiFacets#getAllDims, the default DrillSideways#buildFacetsResult, the inherited Facets#getTopDims) | medium | MultiFacets.getAllDims, the Facets of every DrillSideways result with a drill-down dim, lists the mapped (sideways) dims first in HashMap order instead of sorted by value and returns null for a mapped dim without hits, so the inherited getTopDims returns the wrong dims |
| [LFV-425](LFV-425/) | facet / sampling (RandomSamplingFacetsCollector.amortizeFacetCounts x FacetsConfig drill-down term indexing) | medium | RandomSamplingFacetsCollector.amortizeFacetCounts caps each amortized label count at the docFreq of the label's drill-down term, so with a non-default FacetsConfig DrillDownTermsIndexing (NONE on any dimension, or FULL_PATH_ONLY / DIMENSION_AND_FULL_PATH on a hierarchical dimension) it silently returns 0, or a value below the true count |
| [LFV-723](LFV-723/) | facet / taxonomy facets (TaxonomyFacets#rollup dimension enumeration vs the overridable FacetsConfig default dimension config; FastTaxonomyFacetCounts, TaxonomyFacetFloatAssociations) | medium | Taxonomy facets never roll up a single-valued hierarchical dimension whose config comes from an overridden FacetsConfig#getDefaultDimConfig, so its dimension and non-leaf counts are 0 and getTopChildren returns null or partial results |
| [LFV-367](LFV-367/) | facet / DrillDownQuery (clone and the per-dimension BooleanQuery.Builder state) | medium | DrillDownQuery.clone shares the per-dimension BooleanQuery.Builder objects with the original, so adding a value to a dimension both copies already constrain changes what the other copy matches (released since 4.7.0) |
| [LFV-900](LFV-900/) | core / search collectors (PositiveScoresOnlyCollector; FilterCollector scoreMode and setWeight forwarding; TotalHitCountCollector Weight#count shortcut) | medium | PositiveScoresOnlyCollector inherits its wrapped collector's scoreMode and setWeight, so a wrapped sort-by-field TopFieldCollector or TotalHitCountCollector silently gets the wrong hits or count instead of exactly the hits that score &gt; 0 (score-mode leg since 5.1.0, count leg since 9.3.0) |
| [LFV-690](LFV-690/) | misc / search: DiversifiedTopDocsCollector and DiversifiedTopDocsCollectorManager (per-key diversity cap) | medium | DiversifiedTopDocsCollector and its manager put every document without a key into key 0's group, so a keyless document uses up key 0's maxHitsPerKey quota and real key-0 hits are silently dropped from the top hits |
| [LFV-155](LFV-155/) | misc / DocValuesStats double statistics (DocValuesStatsCollector, DocValuesStatsCollectorManager) | medium | DoubleDocValuesStats and SortedDoubleDocValuesStats (lucene/misc) start max at Double.MIN_VALUE, the smallest positive double, so max returns 4.9E-324 instead of the real maximum whenever every collected value is &lt;= 0 (and min returns Double.MAX_VALUE when every value is +Infinity) |
| [LFV-882](LFV-882/) | misc / DocValuesStats long statistics (DocValuesStatsCollectorManager concurrent reduce) | medium | DocValuesStatsCollectorManager merges LongDocValuesStats and SortedLongDocValuesStats (lucene/misc) through their wrapping long sum, so on a searcher with an executor mean, variance and stdev are silently wrong (for example about 8x too small, or negative) once a slice's long sum or the reduce's running long sum passes Long.MAX_VALUE, as 6 epoch-nanosecond timestamps do, while the same search without an executor is exact |
| [LFV-547](LFV-547/) | facet / taxonomy association facets (TaxonomyFacetIntAssociations, TaxonomyFacetFloatAssociations incl. the DoubleValuesSource path; MAX start value in the per-ordinal, roll-up and dimension aggregates) | medium | Association facets with AssociationAggregationFunction.MAX report 0 instead of the maximum when every value of a label, of a single-valued dimension or of a rolled-up hierarchy node is negative, because the maximum starts from 0 (int, float and DoubleValuesSource paths; every release since 9.2.0) |
| [LFV-382](LFV-382/) | classification / document classifiers (SimpleNaiveBayesDocumentClassifier field boosts) | medium | SimpleNaiveBayesDocumentClassifier applies a documented 'field^boost' (e.g. 'title^10') only on its first call: it writes the stripped field name back into the caller's textFieldNames array, so every later classification silently returns the unboosted result (released since 6.0.0) |
| [LFV-684](LFV-684/) | classification / k-NN classifiers and BooleanPerceptronClassifier (training query) | medium | KNearestNeighborClassifier, KNearestFuzzyClassifier, KNearestNeighborDocumentClassifier and BooleanPerceptronClassifier add their documented training filter query (and the class-field restriction) as scoring Occur.MUST clauses, so a training query that filters out nothing changes the k-NN class scores and can flip the assigned class, the nearest neighbours or the learned perceptron model (released since 4.6.0) |
| [LFV-680](LFV-680/) | classification / BooleanPerceptronClassifier (batched training) | medium | BooleanPerceptronClassifier with batchSize &gt; 1 can silently lose the weight updates of misclassified training documents: the weight FST is rebuilt only when a misclassified document sits at a multiple of batchSize and never after the last batch, and updates inside a batch are computed from the last FST build and overwrite each other, so the trained model is wrong (released since 5.0.0) |
| [LFV-801](LFV-801/) | classification / KNearestFuzzyClassifier (textFieldNames 'field^boost' syntax; NearestFuzzyQuery field lookup) | medium | KNearestFuzzyClassifier ignores its documented 'field^boost' syntax: an entry such as 'title^10' in textFieldNames is looked up as a literal field name, so that field contributes nothing, and with a single boosted field assignClass returns null |
| [LFV-391](LFV-391/) | classification / SimpleNaiveBayesClassifier (training query; copies in SimpleNaiveBayesDocumentClassifier and CachingNaiveBayesClassifier) | medium | SimpleNaiveBayesClassifier applies its documented training query only to the per-word counts: the class prior and the likelihood denominators are computed over the whole index, so labelled documents outside the training set can decide the class (released since 4.6.0; same pattern in SimpleNaiveBayesDocumentClassifier and CachingNaiveBayesClassifier) |
| [LFV-380](LFV-380/) | classification / document classifiers (SimpleNaiveBayesDocumentClassifier.assignClass and getClasses) | medium | SimpleNaiveBayesDocumentClassifier returns NaN for every class and null from assignClass when the document has a field value with no tokens (empty, stop words only or punctuation only), because each value's log-likelihood is divided by its token count (released since 6.0.0) |
| [LFV-681](LFV-681/) | classification / utils: DatasetSplitter (split, createNewDoc fieldNames branch) | medium | DatasetSplitter.split with explicit fieldNames copies only the first value of each multi-valued field into the split indexes |
| [LFV-683](LFV-683/) | classification / utils: DatasetSplitter (split: class count used as the GroupingSearch group limit) | medium | DatasetSplitter.split can silently leave a whole labelled class out of all three output indexes when some documents have no class value, and throws NullPointerException, leaving the output writers locked, when a segment has no class field |
| [LFV-395](LFV-395/) | classification / BooleanPerceptronClassifier (weights FST) | medium | BooleanPerceptronClassifier adds its term weights to its FST in Java String (UTF-16) order instead of the UTF-8 byte order the FST requires, so a text field with a term starting with a supplementary character (such as an emoji) and a term starting with a character in U+E000..U+FFFF (such as a fullwidth letter) makes training throw AssertionError with -ea and, with -da, silently loses term weights (in some term layouts all of them) or throws ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException (released since 5.0.0) |
