| [LFV-514](LFV-514/) | analysis / phonetic (PhoneticFilter and DaitchMokotoffSoundexFilter output from the bundled commons-codec); release notes (lucene/CHANGES.txt, lucene/MIGRATE.md) | low | Bundled commons-codec upgrades changed phonetic filter tokens (RefinedSoundex in 10.2.0; Metaphone, ColognePhonetic and Daitch-Mokotoff on main), and neither CHANGES.txt nor MIGRATE.md mentions it or asks for re-indexing (documentation defect) |
| [LFV-870](LFV-870/) | analysis-common / synonym.word2vec (Word2VecSynonymFilter minAcceptedSimilarity javadoc vs Word2VecSynonymProvider threshold test) | low | Word2VecSynonymFilter documents minAcceptedSimilarity as a cosine similarity, but Word2VecSynonymProvider compares it with the normalised score (1 + cosine) / 2, so the effective cosine cutoff is 2m - 1 (the default 0.8 accepts cosine 0.6, 0.5 accepts orthogonal terms, values below 0.5 accept negatively correlated terms); documentation defect, the javadoc fix is the default proposal |
| [LFV-351](LFV-351/) | facet / facet sets (MatchingFacetSetsCounts child counts vs. FacetSets.adoc and the demo javadoc) | low | FacetSets.adoc says MatchingFacetSetsCounts counts the documents each FacetSetMatcher matches, but it counts matching facet sets, so one document can add more than 1 to a matcher (documentation defect; the per-set code is intended and pinned by upstream tests) |
| [LFV-914](LFV-914/) | monitor / batch matching: Monitor.match(Document) and DocumentBatch (documentation) | low | Monitor.match(Document) does not document that a batch is matched as one index, so queries whose rewrite or statistics depend on the index (top-N multi-term rewrites incl. the default FuzzyQuery, CommonTermsQuery, MoreLikeThisQuery) can match a document differently in a batch than alone |
| [LFV-830](LFV-830/) | queries / intervals (Intervals.within / Intervals.notWithin and their javadoc; queryparser fn:within / fn:notWithin docs) | low | Intervals.within and notWithin are documented as opposites, but a multi-position source interval only partly inside the n-position window around the reference matches neither (documentation / API-contract defect) |
| [LFV-903](LFV-903/) | core / search similarities (MultiSimilarity: undocumented norm ownership, inherited getDiscountOverlaps) | low | MultiSimilarity does not document that only sims[0] computes the field norm, so the other sub-similarities' index-time norm settings (discountOverlaps, a computeNorm override) are silently ignored and the array order decides the stored norm, and its inherited getDiscountOverlaps always returns true (documentation defect; on one index the score is an exact sum in either order) |
| [LFV-812](LFV-812/) | index / IndexWriter delete APIs (deleteAll and deleteDocuments(Query...) javadoc) | low | IndexWriter.deleteAll javadoc says deleteDocuments(MatchAllDocsQuery) is slower and only marks documents deleted, but since 5.2.0 (LUCENE-6379) deleteDocuments(Query...) runs an exact MatchAllDocsQuery as deleteAll, which also resets the field schema (javadoc only; the routing is intended) |
| [LFV-716](LFV-716/) | queryparser / ext: Extensions, ExtendableQueryParser (default extension-field encoding javadoc) | low | Extensions javadoc says splitExtensionField inverts buildExtensionField, but the default split cuts at the first delimiter and nothing says the delimiter must not occur in field names, so with the '_' delimiter of the ExtendableQueryParser example a field like first_name silently never reaches its extension (documentation defect; the first-occurrence split itself is documented and intended) |
| [LFV-529](LFV-529/) | facet / taxonomy (TaxonomyFacetLabels, per-document facet label retrieval) | low | TaxonomyFacetLabels javadoc offers it as a replacement for stored fields, but for dimensions configured multi-valued plus hierarchical (or multi-valued plus requireDimCount) it also returns the ancestor paths and the bare dimension that FacetsConfig indexes only for counting (documentation/contract defect; every release since 8.7.0) |
| [LFV-536](LFV-536/) | analysis-common / ConcatenateGraphFilter and ConcatenateGraphFilterFactory (maxGraphExpansions javadoc); also suggest / CompletionAnalyzer | low | ConcatenateGraphFilter, its factory and CompletionAnalyzer misdocument maxGraphExpansions: the javadoc promises a TooComplexToDeterminizeException when the token graph has more than maxGraphExpansions paths (and 'no limit on graph expansions' for CompletionAnalyzer's 1- and 3-argument constructors), but the code silently keeps the first maxGraphExpansions paths (default 10000) and also uses the same value as the determinize work limit, so it can throw for a graph with fewer paths than the limit (documentation defect; the truncation is intended) |
| [LFV-885](LFV-885/) | core / index merge scheduling (MultiIndexMergeScheduler visibility) | low | MultiIndexMergeScheduler is listed under Lucene 10.3.0 New Features as 'a production multi-tenant merge scheduler' and its javadoc says to create one instance per index, but the class is package-private, so application code cannot compile against it or construct it through supported means |
| [LFV-702](LFV-702/) | analysis-nori / KoreanPartOfSpeechStopFilterFactory (javadoc example of the stop tags) | low | KoreanPartOfSpeechStopFilterFactory's class javadoc example tags="E,J" fails with IllegalArgumentException ('No enum constant org.apache.lucene.analysis.ko.POS.Tag.E') since 10.0.0: GITHUB#11778 replaced the POS tags E and J with fine-grained tags and updated the factory test, but not the javadoc example, and no CHANGES or MIGRATE note survives (documentation defect; the tag change is intended) |
| [LFV-930](LFV-930/) | analysis-common / SimplePattern(Split)TokenizerFactory javadoc examples; core / util.automaton RegExp error message | low | SimplePatternSplitTokenizerFactory and SimplePatternTokenizerFactory javadoc whitespace examples (pattern="[ \t\r\n]+" and "[^ \t\r\n]+" in an XML attribute, so the backslashes stay literal) never split on whitespace: since 9.0.0 RegExp rejects them with IllegalArgumentException (the rejection is intended, LUCENE-9370), on 6.5 to 8.x they split on the letters t, r, n; the RegExp error also prints the rejected letter as a decimal code point ('invalid character class \116' for \t) |
| [LFV-797](LFV-797/) | facet / taxonomy (DirectoryTaxonomyReader.getBulkPath: side effect on the ordinals argument of the category-cache path added by #15711) | low | DirectoryTaxonomyReader.getBulkPath overwrites and duplicates entries of its ordinals argument when a cached ordinal comes before an uncached one (since 10.5.0), although the TaxonomyReader javadoc says the method may only reorder them (documentation/contract defect; the returned labels are correct) |
| [LFV-907](LFV-907/) | search / CachingCollectorManager lifecycle (documentation; new public API in 10.6.0) | low | CachingCollectorManager (new in 10.6.0) does not document that it is single-use: reused for a second search, replay replays the hits of both searches and one earlier overflow keeps isCached false |
| [LFV-890](LFV-890/) | index / deletion policies (SnapshotDeletionPolicy and PersistentSnapshotDeletionPolicy restart lifecycle, javadoc) | low | A PersistentSnapshotDeletionPolicy re-opened over its save file counts the persisted snapshots, but getSnapshots is empty and getIndexCommit(gen) returns null until an IndexWriter has opened with it, and no javadoc states this precondition (documentation gap; the behaviour is by design) |
| [LFV-931](LFV-931/) | analysis-common, analysis-kuromoji, analysis-phonetic, analysis-opennlp / analysis factories (class javadoc &lt;fieldType&gt; schema examples) | low | Eight analysis-factory class javadoc schema examples cannot be used as written: six are not well-formed XML (StopFilterFactory, JapaneseIterationMarkCharFilterFactory, BeiderMorseFilterFactory, OpenNLPTokenizerFactory, OpenNLPLemmatizerFilterFactory, DropIfFlaggedFilterFactory) and two name a class that is not a factory (LimitTokenOffsetFilterFactory's solr.LimitTokenOffsetFilter, PatternTypingFilterFactory's com.example.PatternTypingFilter) (documentation defect) |
| [LFV-530](LFV-530/) | suggest / spell (HighFrequencyDictionary threshold) | low | HighFrequencyDictionary javadoc says terms appearing in less than thresh of the documents are excluded, but the code rounds thresh * numDocs down and keeps them (a term in 1 of 3 documents passes thresh 0.5); documentation/contract defect, the javadoc fix is the default proposal |
| [LFV-122](LFV-122/) | search / sorting (SortedNumericSortField missing values; javadoc) | low | SortedNumericSortField's class javadoc on main says missing values sort first or last via SortField.STRING_FIRST / STRING_LAST, but a numeric sort cannot use these string sentinels: the constructor accepts them, then every search or index sort with them throws ClassCastException |
| [LFV-537](LFV-537/) | suggest / spell (JaroWinklerDistance threshold) | low | JaroWinklerDistance.setThreshold javadoc says 'Set to a negative value to get the Jaro distance', but a negative threshold adds the Winkler bonus to every pair (the same as threshold 0); only a threshold of 1 or more gives plain Jaro; documentation defect, the javadoc fix is the default proposal |
| [LFV-156](LFV-156/) | index / ImpactsEnum shallow-advance contract (ImpactsSource javadoc, test-framework AssertingImpactsEnum); codecs / Lucene104 postings reader | low | ImpactsSource#advanceShallow javadoc does not say that the current document's freq, positions and docIDRunEnd become invalid after a shallow advance past its block: the default codec then returns another block's data, and a lazy freq breaks the next legal advance (javadoc and assertion gap; the codec behaviour is intended) |
| [LFV-149](LFV-149/) | search / kNN visit limit (visitedLimit javadoc on LeafReader, TopKnnCollector and the kNN collector managers; HNSW and exhaustive vector search) | low | The kNN visitedLimit javadoc (LeafReader#searchNearestVectors, TopKnnCollector, KnnCollectorManager, TopKnnCollectorManager, HnswGraphSearcher) calls it 'the maximum number of nodes that the search is allowed to visit', but since 10.3/10.4 batched scoring deliberately treats it as a best-effort limit that a search can exceed (javadoc only; the code behaviour is intended upstream) |
| [LFV-119](LFV-119/) | index / SegmentInfos version check (javadoc) | low | SegmentInfos#readLatestCommit(Directory, int) and #readCommit(Directory, String, int) javadocs misdescribe the minimum-version check: readLatestCommit still names the index-created major, and both say 'strictly greater' although the check is inclusive (javadoc only; the code is correct) |
| [LFV-497](LFV-497/) | index / IndexReader reference counting (decRef javadoc) | low | IndexReader#decRef javadoc says 'If an exception is hit, the refCount is unchanged', but since LUCENE-5553 (4.7.1) a final decRef whose doClose or closed listener throws leaves the reader closed at refCount 0 (documentation defect; the code behaviour is intended and must not change) |
| [LFV-328](LFV-328/) | util / ArrayUtil (hashCode(char, int, int) javadoc) | low | ArrayUtil.hashCode(char, int, int) javadoc says the end index is inclusive, but the code and its only caller treat it as exclusive (documentation defect; the code is correct and must not change) |
| [LFV-943](LFV-943/) | index / SegmentInfos (segments_N file-format javadoc vs its writer and reader) | low | The SegmentInfos class javadoc gives a wrong segments_N (commit point) grammar: IndexCreatedVersionMajor, SoftDelCount, the SegmentCommitInfo id and FieldInfosFiles are missing, NameCounter is documented as an Int32 but written as a VLong, the fixed-width integers are written big-endian although the javadoc links the little-endian DataOutput#writeInt/#writeLong, and SegID is called the codec's identifier (documentation defect; the writer and reader agree) |
| [LFV-942](LFV-942/) | codecs / field infos (Lucene94FieldInfosFormat.fnm file-format javadoc vs its writer and reader) | low | The file-format javadoc of the default field infos format (Lucene94FieldInfosFormat) gives a wrong.fnm grammar: the per-field sequence leaves out IndexOptions, the doc-values skip-index byte, PointIndexDimensionCount and the three vector bytes, and the value tables miss written values (doc-values codes 4 and 5, FieldBits 0x8 and 0x10, IndexOptions 5 since 10.5.0, VectorEncoding 2 on main) (documentation defect; the writer and reader agree) |
| [LFV-934](LFV-934/) | codecs / segment info (Lucene99SegmentInfoFormat.si file-format javadoc vs its writer and reader) | low | The file-format javadoc of the default segment info format (Lucene99SegmentInfoFormat) gives a wrong.si grammar: SegVersion and SegMinVersion are typed as String, but the writer stores three Int32s (and a flag byte plus three Int32s), and SegMinVersion and HasBlocks are missing from the documented sequence (documentation defect; the writer and reader agree) |
| [LFV-939](LFV-939/) | codecs / terms dictionary (Lucene103BlockTreeTermsWriter.tim/.tmd file-format javadoc vs its writer and reader) | low | The file-format javadoc of the default terms dictionary (Lucene103BlockTreeTermsWriter) gives a wrong.tim/.tmd grammar: each.tmd FieldStats still lists RootCodeLength and root-code bytes, which the trie-based terms index no longer writes, and the PostingsHeader is documented at the start of every.tim field although it is written once in.tmd, before NumFields (documentation defect; the writer and reader agree) |
| [LFV-938](LFV-938/) | codecs / norms (Lucene90NormsFormat.nvm file-format javadoc vs Lucene90NormsConsumer / Lucene90NormsProducer) | low | The file-format javadoc of the default norms format (Lucene90NormsFormat) gives a wrong.nvm grammar: each Entry omits the Int16 jump-table entry count and the byte dense rank power that the writer stores right after DocsWithFieldLength, so a parser that follows it misreads NumDocsWithField and every later field; the grammar line is also labelled '.dvm' (documentation defect; the writer and reader agree) |
| [LFV-941](LFV-941/) | codecs / vectors (Lucene99FlatVectorsFormat.vemf and Lucene99HnswVectorsFormat.vem and.vex file-format javadoc vs their writers and readers) | low | The file-format javadocs of the default vector formats (Lucene99FlatVectorsFormat and Lucene99HnswVectorsFormat) give a wrong.vemf/.vem/.vex grammar: both metadata lists leave out the int32 vector encoding that is written right after the field number, so every later element is misread, and since 10.3 the.vex list still calls the HNSW neighbour deltas vints although they are group-varint encoded (documentation defect; the writers and readers agree) |
